4195               	; 	'
4196               	; 	'   spawn      escape     round
4197               	; 	'   screen 3   119.9 s    100 s   -- impossible
4198               	; 	'   screen 5   108.1 s    100 s   -- impossible
4199               	; 	'   screen 6   102.3 s    100 s   -- impossible
4200               	; 	'   screen 7    96.4 s    100 s   -- 3.6 s of room
4201               	; 	'

4203               	; 	' along floor 3 to that one, then east again along the roof to the door
4204               	; 	' -- so a screen further west is paid for three times over: about six
4205               	; 	' seconds each, against 3.6 to spare. Starting him at the lift would
4206               	; 	' mean he could never escape at all, and one of the two loss conditions
4207               	; 	' would quietly stop existing.

4377               	; 	' HARRY'S SPEED IS IN QUARTER PIXELS, AND IT DOES NOT RAMP. It used to be
4378               	; 	' 2 px/frame against Kelly's 3, which sounds like a comfortable 1.5x --
4379               	; 	' and Kelly still lost the race to the roof by ELEVEN SECONDS, every
4380               	; 	' round, because the two routes are not the same length. Kelly runs
4381               	; 	' 7,992 px (three traverses plus the roof); Harry runs 4,104, because he
4382               	; 	' spawns beside floor 2's escalator and so skips a traverse. A chase
4383               	; 	' resolves on PATH / SPEED, and Kelly needs to be more than TWICE
4384               	; 	' Harry's speed before he is ahead at all. See DESIGN.md 4a.
4385               	; 	'
4386               	; 	' 1.75, AND THE NUMBER IS MEASURED, NOT GUESSED. Tracked off the
4387               	; 	' reference video (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen
4388               	; 	' widths a second and its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At 1.5 ours
4389               	; 	' was 2.67 and the crook visibly strolled. 1.75 brings it to 2.29.
4390               	; 	'
4391               	; 	' 2.0 would match the original exactly and still cannot be used, because
4392               	; 	' OUR routes are not the original's: Kelly's is 1.95x Harry's here, so a
4393               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves him 5.2 s ahead at the escape edge -- less
4394               	; 	' than one beach ball (9 s). At 1.75 he is 10.1 s ahead, which absorbs a
4395               	; 	' hit. That figure counts the LIFT, which is on his way and carries him
4396               	; 	' two floors; the foot-only route is 5 s worse and is not the route
4397               	; 	' anybody takes.
4398               	; 	'
4399               	; 	' THE PER-KROOK DIAL IS STILL NOT HIS SPEED. It is the obstacles, which
4400               	; 	' is also the only dial the manual names (`obsp` above, and the ball
4401               	; 	' arcs). assets/checkchase.py checks all of this mechanically -- run it
4402               	; 	' if you touch these numbers.

4430               	;
4431               	; 	' ======================================================================
4432               	; 	' DRAWING THE STORE -- four blits, once per screen crossing
4433               	; 	' ======================================================================
4434               	; 	' This is the whole cost of the flip, and it is paid on a discrete event
4435               	; 	' rather than every frame. RallyX's pan repaints 576 chars every time the
4436               	; 	' camera moves one cell; this repaints 640 about once every two seconds
4437               	; 	' of running.
4438               	; 	' SPRITES OFF BEFORE THE BLIT, NOT AFTER IT. This routine WAITs between
4439               	; 	' bands -- one band a frame, because a burst of VDP writes past a few
4440               	; 	' dozen in one frame is silently dropped -- so painting the new screen
4441               	; 	' takes several frames, and for all of them the OLD screen's actors and
4442               	; 	' obstacles were still sitting on top of it. Following Harry through a
4443               	; 	' seam showed him and every cart from the floor he had just left,
4444               	; 	' standing on a shop that was assembling itself underneath them.
4445               	; 	'
4446               	; 	' They are put back by the normal draw on the same pass, so the crossing
4447               	; 	' reads as a cut rather than as a dissolve with ghosts in it.
4448               	; 	' EACH BAND IS FINISHED BEFORE THE FRAME IT IS DRAWN IN ENDS, and the
4449               	; 	' bands go TOP DOWN. Both halves of that matter.
4450               	; 	'
4451               	; 	' The blit paints the template -- shelves, pillars and all -- and the
4452               	; 	' radios and prizes then take out the fixtures they stand in. Those two
4453               	; 	' used to be four frames apart: all four bands blitted with a WAIT
4454               	; 	' between them, and only afterwards the clearing. So every screen change
4455               	; 	' showed its counters and beams for several frames and then erased them,
4456               	; 	' which reads as the shop flickering rather than as it being drawn.
4457               	; 	'
4458               	; 	' TOP DOWN because a band's beam tops are stamped into the SLAB ROW OF
4459               	; 	' THE BAND ABOVE. Going bottom-up, band 1's blit would paint over the
4460               	; 	' beam tops band 0 had just put there. Going top-down the band above is
4461               	; 	' always already drawn, so everything a band needs -- its own cells and
4462               	; 	' the row it borrows overhead -- is final by the time its WAIT arrives.
4463               	; 	'
4464               	; 	' `3 - dq` rather than `FOR dlv = 3 TO 0 STEP -1`: dlv is an unsigned
4465               	; 	' byte, so counting down past zero wraps to 255 and the loop never ends
4466               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A).
4467               	; draw_screen:
4468               cvb_DRAW_SCREEN
4469               	; 	GOSUB hide_play
4470 B9F8 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B9FA A980

4484               	; 		' at round start, after CLS, that reads as a support beam appearing

4577               	;
4578               	; 	' A SUPPORT BEAM HAS TO REACH THE FLOOR IT HOLDS UP, AND THAT FLOOR IS
4579               	; 	' NOT IN ITS OWN BAND. A beam fills its band's four air rows, so its top
4580               	; 	' pixel sits under the slab row of the band ABOVE -- and a slab row is
4581               	; 	' five pixels of bar over three of green (see SLAB), so the beam stopped
4582               	; 	' three pixels short of the bar on every storey. It read as a beam that
4583               	; 	' misses the floor, which is exactly what it was.
4584               	; 	'
4585               	; 	' It cannot be drawn into either template. The beam belongs to the band
4586               	; 	' below and the row belongs to the band above, and the two templates are
4587               	; 	' chosen independently per screen, so no template knows both. So the
4588               	; 	' band blits go down first and the tops are stamped afterwards, from a
4589               	; 	' per-template column table -- the same shape as the escalator's head cap.
4590               	; 	'
4591               	; 	' Bands 0 and 1 only. Band 2's top row is the ROOF DECK, which is grey
4592               	; 	' over grey with no green to bridge, and the roof itself has no band
4593               	; 	' above it at all.
4594               	; 	' BANDS 0 TO 2 -- ALL THREE SHOPPING FLOORS. It used to stop at 1,
4595               	; 	' because band 2's ceiling is the ROOF DECK and the deck had no green
4596               	; 	' under it: a beam there already ran straight into grey and needed
4597               	; 	' nothing. Giving the roof its three green rows (so the lift shaft stops
4598               	; 	' under it, like every other floor) took that away, and the top floor's
4599               	; 	' beams started falling three pixels short.
4600               	; 	'
4601               	; 	' So the roof gets its own version of the stamp. A beam HOLDS THE ROOF
4602               	; 	' UP and must reach it; the shaft merely stops beneath it.
4603               	; beam_one:

     C084 2264     
5318 C086 D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5319               	; 			' THE BALL IS ON THE GROUND WHEN HE GETS TO IT.
5320               	; 			'
5321               	; 			' The bounce phase used to be `lx AND 31` -- a byte out of
5322               	; 			' the placement table, which is to say arbitrary. Run onto
5323               	; 			' a screen and the first ball might be at the bottom of its
5324               	; 			' arc, where a jump clears it, or at the top, where it has
5325               	; 			' to be ducked, and nothing on the way in told you which.
5326               	; 			' Sometimes you could keep running and jump it; sometimes
5327               	; 			' the same approach at the same speed could not be jumped
5328               	; 			' at all. That is not difficulty, it is a coin toss.
5329               	; 			'
5330               	; 			' So the phase is worked BACKWARDS from the meeting. He
5331               	; 			' closes 4 px a pass and the ball 2, so they meet in
5332               	; 			' (gap / 6) passes; the phase advances one a pass and wraps
5333               	; 			' at 32, so seeding it with the NEGATIVE of that lands it on
5334               	; 			' phase 0 -- the ground -- exactly as they arrive.
5335               	; 			'
5336               	; 			' The gap is the same either way round: he enters at one
5337               	; 			' wall and the ball starts `stag` in from the other, so it
5338               	; 			' is 232 - stag whichever direction the floor runs.
5339               	; 			'
5340               	; 			' It stays forgiving rather than exact. The arc is flat near
5341               	; 			' the bottom, so anything within eight passes either side is
5342               	; 			' still under the jump; and it only holds for an unbroken
5343               	; 			' run, which is the case the player is entitled to read off
5344               	; 			' the screen. Duck, stop or take a hit and everything after
5345               	; 			' is out of step again -- which is the game.
5346               	; 			obp(li) = lx AND 31
5347 C088 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LX,r0
     C08A 21D2     
5348 C08C 0201  20 	li r1,7936   ; 31*256
     C08E 1F00     
5349 C090 0541  14 	inv r1
5350 C092 5001  18 	szcb r1,r0
5351 C094 C040  18 	mov r0,r1
5352 C096 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LI,r0
     C098 21C2     
5353 C09A 0980  32 	srl r0,8
5354 C09C 0220  22 	ai r0,array_OBP
     C09E 2274     
5355 C0A0 D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5356               	; 			IF lk = OB_BALL THEN
5357 C0A2 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LK,r0
     C0A4 21C4     
5358 C0A6 0201  20 	li r1,512   ; 2*256
     C0A8 0200     
5359 C0AA 9001  18 	cb r1,r0
5360 C0AC 1302  14 	jeq cv232
5361 C0AE 0460  28 	b @cv231
     C0B0 C0DC

5364 C0B2 D020  34 	movb @cvb_STAG,r0

5366 C0B8 C040  18 	mov r0,r1

6001               	;
6002               	; 	' ONE CELL OF THE DOORWAY, from its column (ccl 0-3), its band row
6003               	; 	' (crw 0-3) and the door state (cst). Four columns and four rows, so
6004               	; 	' sixteen cells, and the three interesting things about a cell are all
6005               	; 	' edges: column 0 and column 3 carry the JAMB, row 0 carries the LINTEL
6006               	; 	' and row 3 the SILL.
6007               	; 	'
6008               	; 	' THE SILL IS REVEALED AS THE DOORS PART, which falls out of this rather
6009               	; 	' than being arranged: shut, every cell is plain door and there is no
6010               	; 	' sill to see; part-open, only the middle two columns show car and sill;
6011               	; 	' open, all four do. A sill sitting under a shut door would be a ledge
6012               	; 	' with nothing behind it.
6013               	; 	'
6014               	; 	' Nested IFs, never `ccl > 0 AND ccl < 3` -- the 9900 backend miscompiles
6015               	; 	' a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
6016               	; car_cell:
6017               cvb_CAR_CELL
6018               	; 	ccw = CH_EDOOR

     C62C 6C00

     C630 20AA     
6021               	; 	' THE SILL IS THERE WHETHER THE DOORS ARE OR NOT. Set before the door
6022               	; 	' states so a shut door gets it, and so do the two OUTER columns of a
6023               	; 	' part-open one -- which the cst=1 branch below never touches. The step
6024               	; 	' is then continuous across all four columns of the opening on every
6025               	; 	' floor, including the three the car is not on. It spans the opening and
6026               	; 	' not the jambs: EJAMBL/EJAMBR sit in the wall column either side and
6027               	; 	' stand on the floor beside the threshold, which is where a door post
6028               	; 	' goes.

     C7D0 C86E     
6240               cv312
6241               	; 		' HE IS STANDING ON A STEP, so he moves at the STEP's rate: 2 px
6242               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
6243               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
6244               	; 		'
6245               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
6246               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
6247               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
6248               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
6249               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
6250               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
6251               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
6252               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
6253               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
6254               	; 		'
6255               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
6256               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
6257               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
6258               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
6259               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
6260               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1
6261 C7D2 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESY,r0
     C7D4 2128

6891               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
6892               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
6893               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
6894               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
6895               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
6896               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
6897               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
6898               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
6899               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
6900               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
6901               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
6902               	; 	'
6903               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
6904               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
6905               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
6906               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at
6907               	; 	' the top of one never lands you on the foot of another.

7466               	;
7467               	; 	' THE VICTROLA, AS CHARACTERS. It never moves, so a sprite spent a
7468               	; 	' scan-line slot on something that can be painted once when the screen is
7469               	; 	' drawn -- and at one 16x16 sprite it could only ever be one colour and
7470               	; 	' one size. Four cells is twice the area for no sprite at all.
7471               	; 	'
7472               	; 	' It stands ON the slab, so it fills the band's rows 2 and 3; row 4 is
7473               	; 	' the floor bar itself and draw_prizes uses row 3 for a collectible,
7474               	; 	' which is why the two never share a screen (genstore keeps prizes off
7475               	; 	' the screens that carry hazards).
7476               	; 	'
7477               	; 	' rdall = 1 paints all four cells, 0 repaints only the top-right one --
7478               	; 	' the pulse. Same address arithmetic either way, so the two cannot drift.
7479               	; 	' A BEAM TOP HANGS OUT OF THE FLOOR ABOVE, and the beam it belongs to has
7480               	; 	' just been taken away. `beam_tops` stamps SLABP -- the floor bar with the
7481               	; 	' support carried up through its lower three pixel rows -- into the slab
7482               	; 	' row above every pillar, and it runs BEFORE the radios and prizes are
7483               	; 	' placed. Clearing a pillar's own cells therefore left three grey pixels
7484               	; 	' dangling from the ceiling with nothing under them.
7485               	; 	'
7486               	; 	' TESTED, NOT ASSUMED: only SLABP is put back to SLAB. The row above the
7487               	; 	' top shopping floor is the ROOF DECK, a different character altogether,
7488               	; 	' and writing a floor bar over it would punch a hole in the roof.
7489               	; 	' A SHELF RUN IS FIVE CELLS WIDE and a radio covers two, so one standing
7490               	; 	' on a counter punched a hole through its middle and left the ends either
7491               	; 	' side -- the same incoherence the dangling pillar had, and worse, because
7492               	; 	' a counter reads as one object. The whole unit goes: walk outward along
7493               	; 	' the band's row 2 while the cell is still a shelf top, clearing it and
7494               	; 	' its bottom half together.
7495               	; 	'
7496               	; 	' Bounded at eight steps. The runs are five, so eight is slack rather
7497               	; 	' than a guess -- and an unbounded walk would run off the end of the row
7498               	; 	' if the name table ever held something unexpected.
7499               	; wipe_shelf:
7500               cvb_WIPE_SHELF
7501               	; 	wsn = 8

7572               	;
7573               	; 	' A PILLAR'S CAP MAY BE CLEARED; AN END WALL'S MAY NOT. beam_one stamps
7574               	; 	' both into the slab row of the band above, but they mean different
7575               	; 	' things. SLABP is the top of a free-standing pillar: a radio or a prize
7576               	; 	' stands in front of the pillar, so leaving its cap up there hangs a grey
7577               	; 	' stub over the fixture and that is what this routine exists to remove.
7578               	; 	' SLABE and ROOFSE are written ONLY at column 0 and column 31 -- read
7579               	; 	' beam_one, they are the two edge cases -- and there they are the
7580               	; 	' BUILDING'S OUTSIDE WALL, carrying its brick bond up through the floor
7581               	; 	' above. Clearing those took the support out from under the storey above
7582               	; 	' at the very edge of the screen, so the second floor visibly rested on
7583               	; 	' nothing.
7584               	; 	'
7585               	; 	' It could only ever show on the ESCALATOR screens, which is exactly how
7586               	; 	' it was reported -- one side, then mirrored on the other. The flight
7587               	; 	' fills the middle of an escalator band, so that screen's radio or prize
7588               	; 	' is pushed right out against the end wall; on every other screen no
7589               	; 	' fixture sits close enough for the two-cell clear at #bca and #bca+1 to
7590               	; 	' reach column 0 or 31.

7644               	;
7645               	; 	' THE SAME DISTINCTION ONE ROW LOWER. A radio or a prize stands in the
7646               	; 	' bottom half of a band, so the two cells above it have to be cleared or
7647               	; 	' the pillar it covers keeps its top half hanging in the air. That clear
7648               	; 	' used to be an unconditional green VPOKE, which is only correct when the
7649               	; 	' cell really is a pillar: at column 0 and 31 it is the OUTSIDE WALL, and
7650               	; 	' on the roof it is the SKYLINE. Painting green over either punched a
7651               	; 	' two-row hole through the building -- and because the band is blitted and
7652               	; 	' then corrected within the same pass, you could watch it happen at round
7653               	; 	' start: the wall went up and was taken straight back down again.
7654               	; 	'
7655               	; 	' So it tests for the thing it is allowed to remove rather than trusting
7656               	; 	' the position. COUNTR is a pillar or a counter; anything else stays.

8572               	;
8573               	; 	' THE QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen (2),
8574               	; 	' so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here and
8575               	; 	' is spent as whole pixels. hsp4 = 6 walks 1,2,1,2 (1.5 px/frame); 7
8576               	; 	' walks 1,2,2,2 over four frames (1.75); 8 is a flat 2. Two IFs, not a
8577               	; 	' loop and not a divide: hacc is under 4 on entry and hsp4 is at most 8,
8578               	; 	' so it can never need a third. `%` and `/` both compile to a real
8579               	; 	' TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this runs every frame.
8580               	; 	'
8581               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
8582               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
8583               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
8584               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
8585               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
8586               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
8587               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
8588               	; 	' STILL RUNNING FROM THE STAIRS -- and this has to come BEFORE the move,
8589               	; 	' not after it. It sat below the two `IF hmv` blocks, where all it could
8590               	; 	' still reach was the animation counter: he faced the right way, played
8591               	; 	' the run cycle and did not travel a pixel. He stood at the foot of the
8592               	; 	' flight looking busy.
8593               	; 	'
8594               	; 	' TWO THINGS END THE RUN, AND BOTH ARE VISIBLE ON SCREEN.
8595               	; 	'
8596               	; 	' THE KOP ARRIVING ON THIS FLOOR OUTRANKS IT. This used to override the
8597               	; 	' flee as well as the climb, so for the whole of the run he held one
8598               	; 	' heading no matter where Kelly was -- and ran straight into him without
8599               	; 	' appearing to notice he was there. The flee above has already worked out
8600               	; 	' which way is away; clearing the run simply lets it stand.
8601               	; 	'
8602               	; 	' AND REACHING THE END WALL ENDS IT. It used to be a count of passes,

8880               	; 	' THE CYCLE ADVANCES BY PIXELS TRAVELLED, NOT BY TIME. This was `+ fdv`,
8881               	; 	' which is a clock -- and Harry's speed is a QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR, so
8882               	; 	' hspd is 0 on a third of his frames. On every one of those the legs took
8883               	; 	' another step while the ground did not move, which is precisely what
8884               	; 	' scrubbing your feet looks like. It is the classic foot-slip: the legs
8885               	; 	' and the world running off two different clocks.
8886               	; 	'
8887               	; 	' Advancing by hspd locks the stride to the floor -- he cannot take a step
8888               	; 	' without covering ground, or cover ground without taking one, at any

9146 DD46 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
9147 DD48 0450  20 	b *r0
9148               	;
9149               	; 	' ======================================================================
9150               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
9151               	; 	' ======================================================================
9152               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
9153               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
9154               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
9155               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
9156               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
9157               	; 	'
9158               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
9159               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
9160               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
9161               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
9162               	; 	' broken.
9163               	; 	'
9164               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
9165               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
9166               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
9167               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
9168               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
9169               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
9170               	; 	'
9171               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
9172               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
9173               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
9174               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
9175               	; coll_obst:
9176               cvb_COLL_OBST
9177               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the
9178 DD4A D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     DD4C 2196

     DD50 0300

     DD58 DD5E

     DFC8 2098     
9474               	; 	' A HIT DOES NOT END A JUMP. This used to force ST_RUN and zero the arc,
9475               	; 	' which dropped him straight down out of mid-air onto whatever he happened
9476               	; 	' to be over -- and since the arc is ballistic and ignores the stick once
9477               	; 	' he is airborne (0l), that read as the game taking the controls away
9478               	; 	' rather than as a hit landing. The penalty is the nine units and the
9479               	; 	' knock flash; where he comes down is still his own arc.
9480               	; 	'
9481               	; 	' A ducking Kop is still stood up, because the crouch is a held pose and
9482               	; 	' there is nothing to interrupt.

9713               	; 	' centres are the left edge plus eight, so the eight cancels in the

9715               	; 	' x 253 before he crosses a seam, and 253 + 8 wraps to 5, so a Kop
9716               	; 	' standing at the far LEFT of the screen measured five pixels to a crook
9717               	; 	' walking off the far RIGHT and arrested him across the whole store.
9718               	; 	'
9719               	; 	' Nothing warned: both are plain 8-bit variables, the wrap is silent, and
9720               	; 	' the catch it produces looks like a generous hitbox rather than like
9721               	; 	' arithmetic. The obstacle test has the same shape but cannot reach it --
9722               	; 	' obx tops out at 240 and klx at XWALL, so neither sum passes 255.

9757               	; 	' KELLY FLASHES WHILE HE IS KNOCKED ABOUT. Hitting an obstacle set a
9758               	; 	' 20-frame `knock` and took nine seconds off the clock, and NOTHING on
9759               	; 	' screen said so -- the time simply went. A flash is the arcade's own
9760               	; 	' idiom for "that hit you", costs one variable, and is readable even

9784               cv728
9785               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
9786               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
9787               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
9788               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
9789               	; 	'
9790               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
9791               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
9792               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
9793               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
9794               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
9795               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
9796               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.
9797               	; 	ky = flry(klv)

9942               	; 		' divide -- `/` compiles to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and
9943               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.

10231               	; 	' HIS ARMS SWING, on the same bit of the animation counter that picks the
10232               	; 	' legs -- so the arm that is forward is the one opposite the leading leg,
10233               	; 	' which is what running looks like.
10234               	; 	' THE ARMS SWING ONCE PER FULL LEG CYCLE, ON BIT 16 -- not on bit 8.
10235               	; 	'
10236               	; 	' Bit 8 is one BEAT, and the legs take four of them (bits 8 and 16). So an
10237               	; 	' arm swing keyed to bit 8 completed twice for every one pass of the legs:
10238               	; 	' the top half of him flapping at double the speed of the bottom half.
10239               	; 	' That is the jig. It is not a drawing problem and no amount of redrawing
10240               	; 	' the legs would have touched it.
10241               	; 	'
10242               	; 	' On bit 16 the arms hold through beats 1-2 and swap for 3-4, which is
10243               	; 	' also the right PHASE: beat 1 leads with one leg and beat 3 with the
10244               	; 	' other, so the arm opposite the leading leg is forward in each -- which
10245               	; 	' is what running looks like.
10246               	; 	' THE TORSO RUNS ON THE SAME TWO BITS AS THE LEGS. With four drawn poses
10247               	; 	' the shoulders and arms swing WITH the stride; the old two-frame body had
10248               	; 	' a clock of its own, which is what read as a flap rather than a run.
10249               	; 	hp = P_HBODY

10325               	; 	' HE IS STRIPED FROM CAP TO HEM. Both stripe colours run the whole upper
10326               	; 	' half, so both boxes span rows 0-15 and neither can be tucked away --
10327               	; 	' affordable only because Kelly's hat moved into a box of its own. His
10328               	; 	' FACE is pushed DOWN instead (hy+3, box 3..18) so it clears the cap
10329               	; 	' rows, where his two stripe boxes plus Kelly's hat and face already
10330               	; 	' make four. Worst line of a meeting: exactly four, nothing dropped.
10331               	; 	' HE RISES A PIXEL ON THE RECOVERY BEATS. A runner is highest at mid-flight
10332               	; 	' and lowest at contact; with the body pinned at a constant y, alternating
10333               	; 	' legs under a motionless torso reads as a dance rather than as travel.
10334               	; 	'
10335               	; 	' One pixel is enough at this size, and it lifts the BODY ONLY -- the legs
10336               	; 	' keep their y, so the planted foot stays on the floor and the figure
10337               	; 	' stretches at the waist instead of hopping. Lifting the legs too would be
10338               	; 	' a jump.
10339               	; 	'
10340               	; 	' Beats 2 and 4 are the recoveries, which is exactly `hanim AND 8` -- the
10341               	; 	' cycle's LOW beat bit. IT MUST MOVE WITH THE BEAT BITS ABOVE IT: on the
10342               	; 	' wrong bit he rises and falls twice per stride, a second clock inside one
10343               	; 	' figure, which is the flapping of DESIGN.md 0j2 in the vertical.
10344               	; 	'
10345               	; 	' Safe because HARRY'S COLLISION HAS NO VERTICAL COMPONENT: he is caught on
10346               	; 	' `same level AND |dx| < CATCHR`. Doing this to Kelly would desynchronise
10347               	; 	' his sprite from the ball and biplane windows in DESIGN.md 5a, which is
10348               	; 	' why it is not done to him.
10349               	; 	' THE WHOLE FIGURE RISES, legs included, rather than the body stretching
10350               	; 	' away from the feet. Two reasons, and the second was not the first plan:
10351               	; 	'
10352               	; 	'   * It is what a run does. Mid-flight BOTH feet are off the ground, so
10353               	; 	'     lifting the legs with the body is the correct pose, not a compromise.
10354               	; 	'   * Lifting the body alone opens a one-pixel gap at the waist, and the
10355               	; 	'     torso's bottom row and the legs' top row do not align across it --
10356               	; 	'     green shows through and he reads as coming apart.
10357               	; 	'
10358               	; 	' It also keeps `hfy = hy - 7` and `hy2 = hy + 16` LITERAL, which is not a
10359               	; 	' cosmetic concern: assets/checkbands.py reads each band's draw offset by
10360               	; 	' parsing those exact expressions. Bobbing a differently-named variable
10361               	; 	' made them unreadable and the gate failed the build -- correctly. A
10362               	; 	' checker that can no longer see what it checks has to fail, not shrug.

10494               	; 	' any scanline (see the note above), so a line carries at most Kelly 2 +

10830 EA86 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10831 EA88 0450  20 	b *r0
10832               	;
10833               	; 	' ======================================================================
10834               	; 	' THE SCANNER
10835               	; 	' ======================================================================
10836               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
10837               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
10838               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
10839               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
10840               	; 	'
10841               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
10842               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
10843               	; 	'
10844               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
10845               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
10846               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
10847               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
10848               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
10849               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
10850               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
10851               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
10852               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
10853               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
10854               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
10855               	; esc_tick:
10856               cvb_ESC_TICK

10879               cv805
10880               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
10881               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
10882               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
10883               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
10884               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
10885               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
10886               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
10887               	; 	'
10888               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
10889               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
10890               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
10891               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
10892               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
10893               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
10894               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
10895               	; 	escp = escp + 1
10896 EAAE D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     EAB0 20B0

10909               	; 	' which cells move -- the step cells and the COMPOSITE copies of them
10910               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
10911               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
10912               	; 	' 7 an east one.
10913               	; 	'
10914               	; 	' THIS IS THE ONLY PER-PASS WORK UNIQUE TO THESE TWO SCREENS, and it is
10915               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once
10916               	; 	' per screen third. It therefore costs three times what the character
10917               	; 	' count suggests, which is why these screens run slower than the rest.
10918               	; 	'
10919               	; 	' THE WEST SCREEN USED TO COST HALF AS MUCH AGAIN, because it carries TWO
10920               	; 	' flights: floor 1's crosses into a shop floor and floor 3's into the
10921               	; 	' roof, so it needed both composite sets -- nine characters against the
10922               	; 	' east's six, 216 bytes a pass against 144. It measured 20 passes a
10923               	; 	' second where the east screen managed 24-26. The two sets are
10924               	; 	' PIXEL-IDENTICAL and are never wanted in the same screen THIRD, so they
10925               	; 	' share their characters now and are told apart by the per-third colour
10926               	; 	' table (see esc_deck_col in setup). Six either side.

11189 ED08 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
11190 ED0A 0450  20 	b *r0
11191               	;
11192               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
11193               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
11194               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
11195               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
11196               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
11197               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
11198               	; 	'
11199               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
11200               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
11201               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
11202               	; 	'
11203               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
11204               	; 	'   +1  GREY    the air of that floor
11205               	; 	'   +2  GREY
11206               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line -- and NOTHING else goes here
11207               	; 	'
11208               	; 	' THE FIRST THREE ROWS ARE THE FLOOR'S AIR AND EVERYTHING LIVES IN THEM:
11209               	; 	' the escalator's three-step diagonal, the lift car, and both actors, all
11210               	; 	' three pixels tall. They are grey in the table, and the Kop and the
11211               	; 	' crook recolour their own CHARACTER as they move (scan_mark) -- which is
11212               	; 	' what lets both of them fill the band instead of getting a pixel row
11213               	; 	' each. Eight pixels of this canvas is half a screen of the store, so
11214               	; 	' they are almost never in one character; when they are, they merge.
11215               	; 	'
11216               	; 	' Shrinking from six rows bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the
11217               	; 	' instrument and four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor
11218               	; 	' above it or the bottom of the screen.
11219               	; 	'
11220               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
11221               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
11222               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
11223               	; scan_furn:
11224               cvb_SCAN_FURN

11496               	;
11497               	; 	' THE FLIGHT IS BLACK, and it has to be written per character because the
11498               	; 	' colour table only knows rows -- the same reason the two actors carry
11499               	; 	' their own colour (scan_mark).
11500               	; 	'
11501               	; 	' This runs BEFORE the markers every tick, which is the whole ordering
11502               	; 	' the radar needs: the lift car is painted first and the flights next, so
11503               	; 	' a Kop or a crook standing on either of them paints over it. Nothing
11504               	; 	' moves aside to make room -- the pixels are simply ORed together and the
11505               	; 	' last colour written wins, so an actor in the lift shaft shows as an
11506               	; 	' actor rather than shifting anywhere.

11641 F060 0450  20 	b *r0
11642               	;
11643               	; 	' Blank the canvas by WRITING zeros rather than copying 384 of them out
11644               	; 	' of ROM. This runs twice a game -- at setup and on a new Krook -- so a
11645               	; 	' loop costs nothing anybody can perceive, and it hands the fixed area
11646               	; 	' back the best part of half a kilobyte, which is the budget that
11647               	; 	' actually binds. Paced in bursts: a few hundred VDP writes in a single
11648               	; 	' frame are silently dropped (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11649               	; scan_wipe:
11650               cvb_SCAN_WIPE
11651               	; 	' THIS ADDRESS IS A CHARACTER CODE IN DISGUISE, AND IT WENT STALE.
11652               	; 	' 4096 is the pattern table's BOTTOM THIRD -- rows 16-23, which is
11653               	; 	' band 0 and the radar together -- and the canvas characters begin at
11654               	; 	' genart's SCAN_FIRST, so the base is 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8. It was
11655               	; 	' written out by hand as 4096 + 160*8 when SCAN_FIRST was 160; art
11656               	; 	' added later moved SCAN_FIRST to 208 and nothing moved this with it,
11657               	; 	' so the wipe zeroed the 48 characters BELOW the canvas and never
11658               	; 	' touched the canvas at all.
11659               	; 	'
11660               	; 	' Two of those forty-eight are STRUCTURE. ENDWALL is the building's
11661               	; 	' outside wall and SLABP is a pillar's cap. Blanking a pattern in ONE
11662               	; 	' THIRD leaves a character that draws correctly everywhere else and
11663               	; 	' falls back to its own background colour here -- so the GROUND FLOOR's
11664               	; 	' outside wall turned into plain green while the identical wall one
11665               	; 	' storey up was perfect, and the ground floor's pillars lost the brick
11666               	; 	' carried up through the bar above them. ENDWALL appears only on
11667               	; 	' screens 0 and 7, which is exactly why it was reported as happening
11668               	; 	' only on the escalator screens, mirrored side to side.
11669               	; 	'
11670               	; 	' The name table was correct the whole time. Probing it said so, four
11671               	; 	' times. It was the GLYPH that was empty, in one third only -- which a
11672               	; 	' probe that copies the character somewhere else to look at it cannot
11673               	; 	' see, because the copy renders from a different third's pattern.
11674               	; 	'
11675               	; 	' renumber.py rewrites this line from genart.SCAN_FIRST, and
11676               	; 	' checkstruct.py fails the build if the two disagree.
11677               	; 	#swa = 5760			' 4096 + SCAN_FIRST*8, the canvas patterns
11678 F062 0200  20 	li r0,5760
     F064 1680     
11679 F066 C800  38 	mov r0,@cvb__SWA
     F068 20DA

11748               	;
11749               	; 	' THE TWO ACTORS ARE SPRITES; EVERYTHING ELSE IS CHARACTERS.
11750               	; 	'
11751               	; 	' That split is what makes the instrument simple. The floor lines, the
11752               	; 	' flights and the lift car do not move relative to the canvas, so they
11753               	; 	' are pixels in the scanner's own characters and cost nothing to keep.
11754               	; 	' The Kop and the crook DO move, and as sprites they need no colour
11755               	; 	' table, no erase and no priority arithmetic -- they draw over the
11756               	; 	' furniture because sprites always do, and nothing shifts to make room.
11757               	; 	'
11758               	; 	' IT ALSO REMOVED THE BLINK. Kelly flashed because a pixel row of this
11759               	; 	' canvas can only carry one colour, so at close range the two markers
11760               	; 	' merged and the blink was the only thing left to tell them apart. Two
11761               	; 	' sprites are two colours wherever they stand.
11762               	; 	'
11763               	; 	' The furniture is drawn ONCE, by scan_furn. It used to be redrawn every
11764               	; 	' tick because erasing a character-based marker ANDed bits out of
11765               	; 	' whatever it was standing on; there is nothing left to rub it away. The
11766               	; 	' car still redraws itself, because the car actually moves.
11767               	; 	' -- the lift car. IT WAS FLICKERING, and the cause was not the drawing
11768               	; 	' but the ERASING: scan_elev cleared its three cells and put them back
11769               	; 	' every tick, whether or not the car had moved, so several times a second
11770               	; 	' there was a window with the pixels off. A sprite has no erase at all --
11771               	; 	' it is placed, and the VDP does the rest -- and slot 26 sits BELOW the
11772               	; 	' two markers, so the Kop and the crook pass over it without either of
11773               	; 	' them having to know the car exists.

12486               	;
12487               	; 	' THE BONUS IS COUNTED OUT, NOT AWARDED. Dropping the whole sum into the
12488               	; 	' score in one frame tells the player a number; ticking the clock down a
12489               	; 	' unit at a time, with the score climbing and a beat under each step,
12490               	; 	' tells them what the number was FOR. It is the pay-off for every second
12491               	; 	' they saved, and every arcade game of this era spends a moment on it.
12492               	; 	'
12493               	; 	' It also costs nothing to get right: the loop already has to run down
12494               	; 	' `tsec`, so the animation IS the calculation, and the repeated-addition
12495               	; 	' note below stops applying -- each step adds one band's worth.
12496               	; 	'
12497               	; 	' x100 for Krooks 1-9, x200 for 10-15, x300 from 16. Two independent
12498               	; 	' sources agree on those bands; the manual's own wording suggests
12499               	; 	' 1-8 / 9-16 / 17+ and DESIGN.md 0 records the disagreement.
12500               	; 	'
12501               	; 	' #bval MUST BE 16-BIT: 300 does not fit in a byte, and a plain variable
12502               	; 	' would silently truncate it to 44 (CLAUDE.md 3A).

     F7B2 F704     
12606               	;
12607               	; 	' `calc_bonus` USED TO LIVE HERE and computed the whole sum by repeated
12608               	; 	' addition, to dodge two hazards at once: `tsec * bmul` is an 8-bit
12609               	; 	' product (a 50-unit capture in the x300 band pays 15,000, six times past
12610               	; 	' a byte), and multiplying properly lands on the MPY/r0 hazard where a
12611               	; 	' 16-bit variable read straight after being multiplied returns the
12612               	; 	' product's high word. Counting the bonus out a unit at a time (above)
12613               	; 	' removes the multiply entirely rather than working around it -- the loop
12614               	; 	' the player watches IS the arithmetic.
12615               	; 	'
12616               	; 	' It also fixed the last-tick case for free: `bn_loop` tests `tsec = 0`
12617               	; 	' on entry, where the old `FOR bi = 1 TO tsec` would have run its body
12618               	; 	' once and paid a full multiplier for no time at all (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12619               	;
12620               	; do_escape:
12621               cvb_DO_ESCAPE
12622               	; 	escapd = 0

     F8E2 AA16     
12754               	;
12755               	; 	' Silence every channel FIRST. SOUND latches, and the per-channel decay
12756               	; 	' counters are not ticked inside this loop -- a tone still sounding when
12757               	; 	' the pause starts would hang for its whole duration, which is the classic
12758               	; 	' CVBasic sticky-audio failure.
12759               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL OFF, AND THE DECAY COUNTERS WITH THEM. The counters are
12760               	; 	' ticked by the main loop, so anything still sounding when the loop stops
12761               	; 	' -- for a capture, a death, the bonus count -- sustains for as long as
12762               	; 	' whatever replaced the loop takes. That is where the long beep over the
12763               	; 	' bonus tally came from: not the tally's own note, which turns itself off
12764               	; 	' after every unit, but a footstep or a hit that was still ringing when
12765               	; 	' Harry was caught.
12766               	; snd_off:
12767               cvb_SND_OFF
12768               	; 	SOUND 0,0,0

12770 F8E6 0202  20 	li r2,>8000

12865               	; 	' ======================================================================
12866               	; 	' SOUND
12867               	; 	' ======================================================================
12868               	; 	' Every effect gets an explicit note-off. SOUND latches -- with no
12869               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
12870               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is
12871               	; 	' written as a falling number and anything over 1023 is silently masked
12872               	; 	' to an unrelated pitch.

13219 FCB6 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
13220 FCB8 0450  20 	b *r0
13221               	;
13222               	; 	' EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS ASSEMBLED INTO BANK 1, so the INCLUDE order
13223               	; 	' is load-bearing and nothing but data may follow it.
13224               	; 	'
13225               	; 	' THE FONT IS NOW IN THE BANK TOO, and it used to be deliberately outside:
13226               	; 	' keeping one readable thing in bank 0 means a bank-selection mistake shows
13227               	; 	' as "text survives, art does not" rather than a uniformly blank screen,
13228               	; 	' which is a genuinely useful thing to have when a bank goes wrong.
13229               	; 	'
13230               	; 	' It was given up for 472 bytes, because the fixed area had 342 left and
13231               	; 	' that diagnostic is worth less than the ability to keep building. Two
13232               	; 	' things make it a fair trade rather than a straight loss:
13233               	; 	'
13234               	; 	'   * `BANK SELECT 1` already runs BEFORE `DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits`
13235               	; 	'     (see setup), so nothing about the read order had to change. If that
13236               	; 	'     ever stops being true the font goes blank at boot, immediately and
13237               	; 	'     unmistakably -- a loud failure, not a subtle one.
13238               	; 	'   * The alternative on the table was banking the 29 PRINT AT literals,
13239               	; 	'     which recovers LESS (about 360 bytes net after the reader routine)
13240               	; 	'     and would blind assets/checklayout.py, whose whole method is parsing
13241               	; 	'     `PRINT AT n,"literal"` to catch a string running past column 31 or
13242               	; 	'     a HUD poke landing inside a label. Trading a diagnostic for bytes is
13243               	; 	'     one thing; trading a build gate for fewer bytes is another.
13244               	; 	#if TI994A
13245               	; 	BANK 1
13246      0344     BANK_0_FREE:	EQU >fffe-$
     **** ****     > .rept

13384               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
13385               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
13386               	; 	'
13387               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
13388               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
13389               	; 	' being small sprites.
13390               	; 	'
13391               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
13392               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
13393               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
13394               	; 	'
13395               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
13396               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
13397               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
13398               	;
13399               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
13400               cvb_SPR_KELLY
13401               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00

14397               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
14398               	; 	' Chars 208-255: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
14399               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
14400               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
14401               	;
14402               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
14403               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
14404               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
14405               	; 	' unreadable.
14406               	;
14407               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then a 3 px band of furniture GREY and a yellow floor line, per 4 px level, on dark green. The Kop and the crook are coloured PER CHARACTER at run time -- see scan_set

14519               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
14520               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
14521               	; 	'
14522               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
14523               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
14524               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
14525               	; 	'
14526               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
14527               	; 	' blit is
14528               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
14529               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
14530               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
14531               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
14532               	; 	'
14533               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
14534               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
14535               	; 	'
14536               	; 	' template ids:
14537               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
14538               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
14539               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
14540               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
14541               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
14542               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF0
14543               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF1
14544               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF2
14545               	; 	'   8 T_ROOF3
14546               	; 	'   9 T_ROOF4
14547               	; 	'   10 T_ROOF5
14548               	; 	'   11 T_ROOF6
14549               	; 	'   12 T_ROOF7
14550               	; 	'   13 T_END_W
14551               	; 	'   14 T_END_E
14552               	;
14553               	; stor_tpl:	' 15 templates x 160 bytes
14554               cvb_STOR_TPL
